# Rails assertion to Rust assertion map

Reference: `78b9b1546bdab4c6c1c9b8ddb94512f661289112`. Each row names an original Rails assertion call, its discriminating Rust assertion and the real test that executes it. Shared helper assertions and repeated loop cases are cited explicitly. These tables retain compiler checks as compiler checks; they do not claim matching exception classes between Ruby and the Rust type system.

## WS14e-101

Rails declaration: `test/system/events_test.rb:102` — scheduling an event announces it in the room with a card members respond from

Executed test: `campfire::bin/campfire controllers::ws14_original_browser_tests::original_browser_ws14e_101`.

The named wrapper runs this exact, byte-identical pinned Capybara declaration and its original helpers against the real Rust router, assets, Turbo, Cable and private fixture DB. Every cited original assertion emits a physical-line execution receipt. The original fixture model setup and DB readbacks share that DB; only external Google exchanges use the original WebMock stubs through the injected Rust Google Client. The wrapper rejects zero tests, skipped tests, any failed assertion and any missing original assertion receipt. Original setup sign-in and every reachable nested helper assertion run byte-identical, and the wrapper validates each helper file:line receipt too.

The named wrapper runs this exact, byte-identical pinned Capybara declaration and its original helpers against the real Rust router, assets, Turbo, Cable and private fixture DB. The named wrapper runs this exact, byte-identical pinned Capybara declaration and its original helpers against the real Rust router, assets, Turbo, Cable and private fixture DB.
